## Document 014: 20260921_投資週報_Fed升息重塑利率格局，科技輪動利多仍在

Source PDF: all report pdf/20260921_投資週報_Fed升息重塑利率格局，科技輪動利多仍在.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-09-21
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 21 日
上週重點訊息
◼ Fed 三年來首次升息，點陣圖顯示年底前再升一碼：Fed 於 9/16 一致決議升息 1 碼，將聯
邦基金利率目標區間提高至 3.75%～4.00%，為 2023 年 7 月以來首次升息。最新利率預測
中，18 名官員有 16 人預期年底前至少仍需再升息 1 碼。最新經濟預測顯示，Fed 上修今明
兩年實質 GDP 成長率並下修失業率預估值，但也預期通膨壓力將比原先估計更為頑強。主席
Warsh 並指出美國經濟、就業、內需與資本投資仍具韌性，金融環境難稱緊縮。

◼ AI 安全疑慮引發半導體股急跌，但科技產業內部仍呈現明顯分化： OpenAI、Anthropic 等
AI 業者高層呼籲放慢 AI 發展速度、強化安全防護，引發市場重新評估 AI 投資成長速度與資
本支出前景，拖累費城半導體指數 9/14 重挫約 5.9%，但軟體股反而上漲。現階段尚不足以
證明 AI 資本支出循環已反轉，資金亦未全面撤離科技產業，而是從估值較高、籌碼較擁擠的
AI 硬體轉向其他科技次產業。
◼ 日本央行決議升息 1 碼，符合市場預期：日本央行 9/18 以 7 比 2 決議將無擔保隔夜拆款利
率由 1.0%調升至 1.25%。此為該行 2024 年來第 6 次升息，符合市場預期，故升息後對風險
資產衝擊有限。BOJ 明言將「繼續升息並調整寬鬆程度」 ，並將審視調整的時點與步調。
市場展望與操作策略
◼ 科技股中期趨勢未見反轉，但短線波動料加劇：儘管 FOMC 9 月升息，點陣圖暗示今年可能
再升一碼，但其對經濟成長率與失業率預期仍朝正向發展，同時股市也止跌上揚，顯示投資

人認為企業獲利成長動能足以抵消利率上揚的負面影響，基本面較佳的科技股仍具資金承
接，惟宜留意股市短線仍可能因地緣政治與經濟數據表現而出現波動。
◼ 中天期美債及投資級債風險報酬相對均衡：Fed 重啟升息且點陣圖暗示年底前或再升息，短天
期公債殖利率直接受到政策利率上修影響 ； 天期過長的債券則持續面臨財政赤字 、 發債供給及
期限溢價上升壓力，利率敏感度偏高。中天期(5~10 年期)美國公債既可取得目前相對較高收
益，亦具適度存續期間，在未來通膨降溫時較有機會受惠於殖利率回落，風險報酬較為均衡。
投資級債亦具較高收益與信用防禦特性，有助平衡投資組合波動。

### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 地緣政治風險:
1)槍口下的談判? 中東局勢詭譎: 伊朗向美方提出七大停戰條件, 但與此同時, 葉門「青年運動」仍大
規模襲擊沙國油槽。美國面臨期中選舉壓力, 短線局勢不致大幅升溫; 惟選後(11/3)不確定性仍高。
2)俄烏衝突升級為俄歐戰爭? 歐洲多國提高安全警戒, 以防範俄羅斯的「混合戰」攻勢。預計北約將
持續加大國防支出, 有利國防類股前景。
• 聯準會跟進升息後 , 市場緊縮預期維持高位 : 聯準會啟動升息後 , 目前市場定價未來 12個月仍有再升
3~4碼空間。預計今年Q4殖利率維持高檔, 升息預期導致殖利率曲線趨平。
• AI需求支撐下, 科技股呈現跌深反彈: 雖AI末世論一度導致科技股賣壓, 惟基本面仍反映相關需求強勁:
1)台灣8月外銷訂單突破千億美元、全年上看1.1兆美元, 2)美光將公布財報, 仍將反映記憶體短缺情形。
2. 後續看點:
• 美國PCE(9/30)、非農(10/2); 川習會(9/24); 美光財報(9/30)。策略思維

1) 股市−短線市場持續波動, 中長期獲利成長仍佳: 市場情緒多空交錯, 並同步面臨高利率導致的評價壓抑效果,
留意短期波動風險; 中長期AI投資循環支撐營收動能、獲利前景, 基本面仍支持中長期多頭趨勢。
2) 債市−升息預期帶動下, 利率維持高檔: 近期美、歐、日三大行同步升息, 市場未來均將持續升息, 將延長利
率higher for longer時長。債市布局逢高鎖利, 中短天期為主、降低槓桿率以減少利率敏感度。
3) 匯市−鷹派Fed帶動美元反彈, 後續維持區間盤整: 聯準會轉鷹, 帶動美元扭轉下半年以來的相對貶勢。惟主
要央行同步升息之下, 美元大幅升值的空間亦相對有限, 預計短線DXY指數於100關卡附近震盪。

支持度反映選情壓力, Taco指數依然偏高 國際油價回落, 但9月月增率仍達17%
資料來源: Bloomberg, RealPolitics, OCMacro 截至2026/9/22。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/23。
• 美伊代理人衝突仍在延續, 不過近期漸有衝突降溫的初步跡象: 伊朗對美國提出七項談判條件, 市場開始預期雙方
保留外交協商空間, 帶動西德州原油一度跌破90美元。
• 隨期中選舉(11/3)進入倒數階段, 川普支持度持續低迷, 市場預期美方將避免衝突進一步升級, 但仍處於對峙狀態。
• 選舉後仍存在高度不確定性, 美方曾多次表態「大規模入侵」, 伊朗方面提出的「停戰、解凍資產、解除海上封鎖、
持有濃縮鈾」等條件與美方立場仍有明顯落差。美伊情勢不確定性仍存, 油價短期雖回落至90~100美元區間震盪,
反映市場交易停火預期; 若後續談判停滯、衝突再度升級, 仍不排除再次上攻百元。

Cathay’s View
Fed模型推估: 核心PCE仍維持僵固性 短期通膨預期較5月高點降溫, 惟中長期預期持續墊高
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/9/22。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/9/22。
• 美伊衝突導致的高油價影響, 仍為短期通膨的主要變數; 但隨著高油價的維持時間延長(9月西德州原油月增17%、
汽油月增6%、柴油月增15%), 民生消費壓力、公共運輸成本同步升高, 不排除將進一步擴散至商品、服務等更廣
泛領域的可能性, 推升第二輪通膨擔憂。
• 以克里夫蘭Fed nowcast模型觀察, 核心PCE自2026年初以來便持續上修, 近期預估核心PCE年增率將落在3.4%附
近。市場預期方面, 1Y1Y通膨預期雖較5月高點回落, 但未來5~10年的通膨預期仍持續墊高。
• 市場目前已大致定價 9月、12月各升息一次 。惟通膨走勢持續偏離下 , 仍需審慎看待Fed提前至10月升息的風險,
乃至於10、12月均上調利率的可能。

Cathay’s View
FOMC會議後, 多數官員持鷹派立場 Fed談話持續偏鷹, 隱含潛在升息空間
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/22。
註: Fed NLP指數轉正代表政策態度轉趨鷹派, 歷史在轉正並持續數月上升的狀態下, 升息或
高利率延續的機率上升, 並領先政策利率調整約1~3季。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/22。
• 9月升息後, 多數Fed官員表態支持進一步升息, 反映Fed態度通膨風險仍維持高度警覺。部分官員已聚焦在能源衝
擊的後續傳導效果、通膨走勢持續偏離Fed目標的擔憂。另方面, 本週公布標普製造業、服務業PMI分別創52個月、
59個月新高, 進一步帶動各天期美債殖利率同步攀升。
• 在經濟成長維持韌性、失業率仍處低檔下, 本輪Fed緊縮更接近「預防性升息」邏輯, 而非景氣過熱所引發的需求
型通膨。然而, 市場升息預期仍偏強勁, 預估今年Q4殖利率維持高檔震盪; 近期殖利率曲線趨平, 惟走勢偏向熊平。
• 債市策略: 布局仍以息收為主, 不宜過度期待資本利得, 並適度縮短天期以降低利率敏感度。

債市｜官員談話支持進一步升息, 預估殖利率維持高檔

升息預期帶動美元指數破百, 料後續走勢維持盤整 市場對央行路徑預期變化, 牽動美元指數表現

美元指數Index
• 市場對日本升息預期偏緩, 明年升息路徑可能不若此前預估的強勁(明年即升息至2%), 導致日圓轉貶。利差與對手
國的共振, 推動美元指數在震盪突破100大關。
• 展望後市, 美元雖成功「破百」, 但基本面制約使其頂多維持「盤堅」格局, 觀察今年2月和5月走勢, 當時正經歷流
動性為王和華許上任前的政策預期修正 。然而當前環境已顯著不同, 各國貨幣政策的「底牌」已被市場充分定價,

再缺乏超預期的刺激下, 博弈空間已大幅收窄。美-歐與美-日之間的「2026/12 央行政策利率差異」維持在高位窄
幅震盪, 這使得FED的腳步並未拉開與歐、日等主要央行的利差差異, 難以再現先前的單邊走勢。

BOJ會議前日圓期貨多單押注劇增, 會後面臨平倉壓力 今年高市任命的兩位委員均反對升息
日圓｜鷹派預期落空, 日本央行升息後日圓不升反貶
• 上週五(9/18)BOJ雖升息一碼, 但日圓不升反貶, 主要原因為(1)兩位今年由高市任命的委員投下反對票 , 顯示未來
升息阻力仍不小(2)植田總裁的會後記者會鷹鴿論述混雜 , 並未出現明顯轉向(3)大量投機部位押注日圓走強 , 期貨
淨多單部位為近一年新高、歷史次高, 市場過度押注BOJ轉趨鷹派的預期落空, 投機部位平倉使後續貶值壓力上升。
• 然而, 日圓上週五晚間貶至158價位後, 隨即有消息稱日本央行正在進行rate check使貶幅收斂, 顯示目前日本官方
阻貶的意願仍高, 但進一步升值的空間仍受限, 預期至今年底前日圓應會維持152-160震盪格局。

Cathay’s View
AI需求蓬勃發展, 輝達出貨量持續升溫 AI發展重心轉向推論, CPU需求將大幅增加
科技｜Muse需求爆紅, 反映AI的發展已從訓練轉向推論
• Meta推出的AI代理人Muse近期快速爆紅, 不僅登上美國App Store免費榜前列, 更重新點燃市場對AI需求持續升溫
的期待。與傳統大型模型以GPU訓練為主不同, AI Agent在實際執行任務時, 需要大量CPU負責推理、任務編排、
資料傳輸與背景運算, 因此AI需求的擴張已開始從GPU進一步延伸至CPU。市場也因此重新上修CPU需求預期, 從
2026年的320億美元, 上升到2030年的2800億美元。
• 從投資角度來看, Muse的意義不只是單一AI應用爆紅, 更重要的是驗證AI Agent可能成為下一階段AI普及的重要入
口。近期Intel、AMD與ARM股價同步大漲, 正反映市場開始重新評價CPU在AI時代的角色, AI硬體投資主線也因此
從GPU進一步擴大至CPU。
資料來源: MS, 截至2026/8。 資料來源: MS, 截至2026/9。

AI需求下, 帶動記憶體與NAND需求大增 記憶體短缺下, 毛利率預估持續攀升
科技｜記憶體短缺下, 美光財報可望依舊亮眼
• 美光將於(9/30)公布最新一季財報, 預估營收由上季414億美元, 進一步攀升至505億美元, 年增率亦超過300%。另
外, 毛利率是觀察重點, 市場預估將從上一季的84.6%再度拉高至86.1%, 研判記憶體狀況仍舊吃緊。
• 從各部門的營收來看, 由於DRAM報價持續在走揚, 加上美光也持續擴廠, 預估該部門營收也將從上季的313億美元,
提高至372億美元。此外, 伴隨大量運算後, 儲存需求也大幅攀升, 預估營收也從上季99億美元, 拉高至122億美元,
年增率高達440%。

AI需求旺盛, 外銷訂單單月突破千億 外銷訂單領先出口
台灣｜AI需求暢旺, 有望驅動外銷訂單與出口訂單續創新高
• 台灣外銷訂單持續展現強勁動能, 8月外銷訂單達1,029.6億美元, 月增5.1%、年增71.4%, 首度突破單月千億美元,
創下歷史新高, 並連續19個月維持正成長。主要動能仍來自AI、高效能運算與雲端需求, 其中資訊通信產品及電子
產品訂單分別年增100.5%及83.9%, 顯示AI供應鏈需求仍在快速擴張。此外, 經濟部也預估9月外銷訂單可望續創
新高, 預估介於1060-1091億美元。
• 由於外銷訂單相較出口更具有前瞻性, 且領先約1-3個月, 隨著外銷訂單屢創新高, 預估台灣全年出口金額有望進一
步上修, 並突破9,000億美元, 也將驅動台灣GDP進一步上調。

• 台股近期重拾漲勢, 電子權值股領軍上攻, AI ASIC、先進封裝、PCB及高速傳輸等族群輪動走強, 加權指數再度創

支撐台股中長期行情的主要動能。
• 值得注意的是, 指數雖位居高檔, 但企業獲利上修速度更快, 使台股本益比反而降至近期低檔, 呈現「股價漲、估值
降」的良性結構。這也意味本波行情並非完全由評價面擴張推動 , 而有企業獲利成長支撐 。短線指數漲多後波動
可能放大, 但若AI需求與企業獲利趨勢延續, 拉回仍可留意布局機會。

• 俄烏戰爭持續超過4年, 歐洲各國雖未「正式參戰」, 但近期波蘭、法國、德國、芬蘭等國已集體拉響安全警報, 全

外延伸。作為回應, 烏克蘭於9/19向俄羅斯發射了超過1000架無人機, 創下開戰以來最大規模無人機襲擊紀錄。

通膨, 推高企業經營成本, ECB升息, 也壓抑股價評價, 國防相關族群陷入盤整, 但在長期基本面走強下仍可分批布
局。

10年美債利率位階仍高, 對新台幣形成壓抑 美元反彈與風險偏好拉鋸, 然而新台幣波動相對穩定
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/23。
註: 統計8/21為美元受debasement交易影響下的波段低點; 各幣別升貶為兌美元換算。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/23。
• 下半年市場聚焦美元debasement、歐亞央行升息、日本官方干預, 推動整體亞幣走升 。但在8月下旬以來, 隨著
市場進一步定價Fed升息路徑, 支撐美元自8/21的低點有所反彈。
• 新台幣波動相對穩定: 隨台股創高(近期外資持續加碼)、油價與美債利率漲勢暫歇, 帶動新台幣回穩。
• 新台幣多空因素並存, 料短期維持區間震盪格局, 主因: 1)Fed偏鷹立場將支撐美債利率續居高位, 支撐美元資產吸
引力。然而, 2)台灣AI供應鏈帶動出口、貿易順差持續擴張, 8月外銷訂單1029.6億美元(年增71.4%)再創單月新高,
並留意臨近季底結匯需求, 為新台幣潛在催化劑。

美中雙方可能會晤的議題 美對中出口關稅稅率 vs 人民幣匯價走勢
中國｜川習二會針對貿易與AI; 人民幣延續偏強走勢, 避免貿易齟齬
• 為因應「川習二會」, 官方在7月政治局會議便定調「發力提效」, 並強調「及時出台增量政策」。不可否認, 若能
打破科技圍堵, 將是極大鼓舞; 然而在美中結構性對抗的大背景下, 雙方最可能的交集, 仍僅止於延長貿易休戰與建
立AI對話機制。因此, 這次會面較高機率維持「格局不變、突破有限」的結果。
• 後續, 10月召開的五中全會將主軸放在黨建與反腐, 以「持之以恆推進全面從嚴治黨」為最核心議程, 預期經濟領
域不會是本次會議焦點。這也解釋, 為何官方在五中全會前對於增量政策, 依然保持克制的態度。
• 誠然, 人民幣主要受美中利率政策等因素影響。但就貿易戰角度而言, 不難看出兩次貿易戰時, 人民幣匯價都較為

溫吞, 也顯示官方在關稅疑慮下, 常以貶值作為緩衝; 反之, 進入協議期, 一方面是不確定性降低、風險偏好轉佳, 再
者, 官方也偏好以升值來降低貿易對手的齟齬, 加上人行長線人民幣國際化政策下, 也有利匯價延續偏強震盪走勢。
資料來源: Wind, 國泰世華銀行整理。 資料來源: Wind, 截至2026/9/23。

資料來源: ECB, 截至2026/9, 國泰世華銀行整理。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
官員談話 消費者信心與價格預期
• 近期官員發言整體偏鷹, 但市場對ECB 10月再升息的定價仍僅約40%, 之所以未將10月升息視為基本情境, 主要在
於目前能源衝擊尚未明顯傳導至服務業及更廣泛的核心通膨 。即使ECB首席Lane預期食品、電力及商品價格將面
臨上行壓力, 但他也坦承截至目前為止, 服務價格外溢效應仍相對有限。
• 此外, 最新歐元區9月消費者信心指數由8月的-15.5下降至-16.5, 不僅結束連續四個月改善趨勢, 也略差於市場預期
的-16.0, 反映能源價格上漲壓制家庭支出與經濟前景信心。綜合而言, ECB內部論調正逐漸朝「higher for longer」
靠攏, 但疲弱的消費信心與尚未全面擴散的核心通膨壓力, 使市場仍保留約60%的機率認為ECB將在10月按兵不動。

除非月底9月通膨數據出乎意料, 方能使10月升息機率進一步上修。

英國利率下移 CBI工業趨勢調查
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/23。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/8。
• BOE內部因應近期通膨反彈而維持3.75%基準利率不變, 且票委投票仍維持 6 比 3 的鷹派防禦姿態, 但實質上QT
模式的微調已有效減輕了短期內英國政府的發債融資成本 。隨著主動縮表計劃至 2027年4月前暫停、部分債券改
由直接轉售給財政部(DMO), 英債殖利率預計將擺脫無序暴漲的極端狀態。
• 疊加剛公布的9月CBI工業價格預期指數從22驟降至12, 反映出實體經濟內部通膨螺旋的生產端報價意願明顯降溫 。
促使近一週英國國債殖利率曲線出現整體顯著下移, 修正了此前過度悲觀的「期限溢價」, 讓原本極其陡峭的曲線
長端結構性回落。展望未來, 政策執行的微調與經濟領先指標的傾斜, 為英國的利率政策供階段性不變的思維支撐,

此即10月會議可能按兵不動。

### Page 1

ab 15 September 2026
Global Research
First Read
Novatek Microelectronics Corp.
2026 Taiwan Summit: PC weakness offset by
continued momentum from major U.S. customer
Q3 revenue still expected to grow 5-6% QoQ despite weaker notebook demand
Novatek attended our 2026 Taiwan Summit on Sep 14th. It reiterates Q326 revenue to
grow  5-6% QoQ, supported by seasonal strength in S/M-sized DDI driven by the iPhone
ramp. SoC revenue is expected to grow slightly QoQ, while LDDI should decline

modestly as TV demand remains flattish and PC demand weakens. Importantly,
management highlights notebook PC has seen a meaningful downward adjustment
recently. While the company had originally expected NB shipments to decline  slightly
QoQ, demand has deteriorated to over 10% decline QoQ, with the impact becoming
visible since mid-August. Nevertheless, Q326F guidance for TV, tablet and automotive
remains unchanged. Looking into Q426, management expects seasonal weakness to

continue, with softer memory-driven ASP increases and lower shipment volume likely
resulting in sequential revenue decline.
GM likely peaked in Q226 and should normalize into H226 and 2027
Management highlighted that Q226 GM expanded to 42% from 39% in Q126, mainly
driven by  favourable product mix and lower-cost of  inventory for memory. Looking
ahead, GM is expected to decline QoQ in Q326F as the inventory benefit disappears and
growth shifts toward S/M-sized DDI and LDDI, which carry lower margins than SoC.

Management expects Q426 GM to further contract QoQ, driven by less favourable mix
and softer memory-driven ASP increases. Further out, memory, foundry and OSAT costs
are expected to continue rising into 2027. While Novatek expects to pass through most
cost increases, management acknowledged that the degree and timing of pass-through
will depend on the  negotiation with its customers.
Major US customer remains the key growth driver for 2027-28
The company reiterated strong visibility from its major U.S. customer and expects

revenue to further grow in 2027. Following entry into iPhone DDI programs in 2024 and
subsequent share gains in 2025-26, Novatek expects to continue benefiting from rising
market share as new iPhone models proliferate. Into 2027-28F,  iPhone-related growth
should moderate as the revenue base becomes larger, while management expects
increasing contribution from  premium LCD panels for Macs, supported by a more visible
roadmap. Beyond the U.S. customer, image SoC remains a bright spot due to strong

surveillance demand and emerging Edge AI applications, although management
acknowledged recent growth rates are unlikely to be sustained.
Valuation: Maintain NEUTRAL with price target of NT$540
We retain NEUTRAL with PT of NT$540. While we continue to expect GM normalization
in H226E and 2027E, we believe its GM should sustain at the high-30% level, rather
than  returning to pre-COVID's low-30%.

Company Description
Novatek is a IC design company spun off from UMC's commercial product division in 1997.
The company is focusing on the analog and mixed signal IC design and leads in the display
driver IC and TV SoC market.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Novatek using a PE methodology.

Upside risks include stronger-than-expected demand for the consumer electronics end-
market, as well as higher market share for OLED DDI.
Downside risks include a weaker-than-expected technology end-market demand recovery
and slower-than-expected inventory digestion.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

## Document 145: 8月宏观数据点评：结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月宏观数据点评：结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现.pdf
Report source: 银泰证券
Date: 2026-09-22
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
宏观研究
宏观点评
结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现
——8 月宏观数据点评
事件：2026 年 9 月 15 日，国家统计局发布 8 月份国内宏观经济数据。8 月
份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.2%，社会消费品零售总额同比增长
0.4%；1—8 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 7.2%，房地产开发
投资同比下降 19.9%。
核心观点：
 数据上看，8 月国内经济延续分化格局。出口高增以及新兴产业快速发展
背景下，工业生产好于预期，新动能领域对全部规模以上工业增加值增
长贡献进一步增强；但与此同时受国内需求持续偏弱，稳增长政策尚未
全面发力等因素影响，社零、固定资产投资增速进一步走低。房地产市
场整体延续筑底格局，但二手房交易网签面积连续多个月高于新房销售
面积，同时一线城市房价企稳趋势继续得到确认，房地产市场结构性亮
点增多。
 8 月国内经济整体增长动能依然偏弱，后期经济表现在很大程度上仍将取
决于政策落地节奏。7 月底政治局会议定调“加快财政支出和债券资金使

用进度”以来，政策面积极信号增多，国家发改委多次召开项目协调会
推进“六张网”建设，中央预算内投资基本下达完毕，地方政府专项债
券的发行使用进度加快，9 月新型政策性金融工具开始全面投放。后期
伴随政策效果显现，国内经济增长动能有望边际改善。
 风险提示：1、政策力度不及预期；2、房地产调整超预期。
2026 年 9 月 21 日

### Page 2

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
1、出口与新动能支撑，工业生产好于预期
8 月份全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.2%，
较上月的 4.5%加快 0.7 个百分点，市场一致预期为
增长 4.7%。1—8 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长
5.3%，与 1—7 月持平。
8 月份工业生产表现好于预期，主要得益于外需
拉动与新兴产业发展的双重支撑。一方面，出口持续
高增为工业生产提供有力支撑。以美元计 8 月份我国
出口同比增长 25%，增速较上月进一步加快；受此带
动，当月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长
11.1%，增速较上月提升 0.7 个百分点，外需对工业
生产的拉动作用持续显现。另一方面，新兴产业快速
发展同样为工业生产注入强劲动能。8 月份，高技术
制造业增加值同比增长 16.7%，装备制造业增加值同

2、社零消费继续呈现偏弱格局
8 月份，全国社会消费品零售总额同比增长
0.4%，增速较上月的 0.6%回落 0.2 个百分点，市场
一致预期为+0.8%。1—8 月份社会消费品零售总额同
比增长 1.1%，较 1-7 月放缓 0.1 个百分点。背后汽
车类带来的拖累依然明显，扣除汽车类商品后的消费
品零售额增长 2.7%，增速与 1—7 月份持平。
按消费类型分，8 月商品零售额增长 0.3%（上月
为+0.5%），餐饮收入增长 1.1%（上月为+1.4%），
增速较上月均有不同程度的放缓。8 月限额以上单位
消费品零售额同比下降 3.7%，自 4 月份以来已连续 5
个月负增长。

## Document 171: AI开始“要脸”了

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI开始“要脸”了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Meta 的 Muse 和 Manus 的 Cue 正推动 AI 从 “ 聊天⼯具 ” 向具备⾯孔、⾝份、记忆和⽀付能⼒的
智能体转变。随着模型能⼒差距缩⼩， AI 竞争正从 “ 谁更聪明 ” 转向 “ 谁更像⼈ ” ，⼈格化成为
提升⽤⼾信任与粘性的关键。同时， AI 深⼊⽣活和交易也带来隐私、平台利益冲突及监管
等新问题。
摘要：
9 ⽉上旬， Meta Connect ⼤会舞台上，扎克伯格展⽰了⼀段不同寻常的交互：他的 Muse 智能
体，⼀个⾝穿托加袍、头戴桂冠、名叫 “Agrippa” 的卡通形象，不仅帮他⽣成了⼀份烘焙配⽅，
还替他下单了所有⻝材。⼆⼗天后， Manus 发布 Cue ，给⾃⼰的 AI 智能体发了邮箱、电话号码和
数字钱包 —— 它能以 “ ⾃⼰ ” 的⾝份发消息、接电话、在预算内⾃主付款。
两家公司，殊途同归。⼀个给 AI ⼀张可爱的脸，⼀个给 AI ⼀个独⽴的⾝份。它们共同指向同⼀个
信号： AI 正在从聊天窗⼝⾥⾛出来，变成⼀个有⾯孔、有名字、有 “ ⽣活 ” 的存在。

这不是⼀次简单的 UI 升级。这是 AI 产品竞争逻辑的根本转向 —— 当底层模型能⼒趋于同质化，⼤
⼚的⽤⼾争夺战正在从 “ 谁更聪明 ” 转向 “ 谁更像⼈ ” 。
AI 不再是⼀团空⽓
过去三年， AI 产品的基本形态是⼀个空⽩对话框。你问，它答。整个交互冷冰冰的 —— 没有⾯
孔，没有语⽓，没有个性，甚⾄连⼀个 “ 名字 ” 都常常被忽略。
Muse 和 Cue 代表了⼀种截然不同的设计哲学。
Muse 的默认形象叫 “Jolly” ，⽶⾊系卡通⾝体，圆圆的⿊眼睛，微微上扬的笑容 —— 被媒体形容
为 “ 像《超能陆战队》⾥的⼤⽩，但更可爱⼀些 ” 。⽤⼾可以为它定制外观、名字和服饰，扎克伯
格⾃⼰的 Muse 就叫 “Agrippa” 。更关键的是，通过 Muse Realtime Avatar 模型，⽤⼾可以跟这个
形象进⾏实时视频聊天， “ 对着⼀个公司 Logo 或抽象实体说话总觉得很奇怪 ” 的设计逻辑被彻底推
翻。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

Manus 的 Cue 选择了另⼀条路。它没有给 AI ⼀张脸，⽽是给 AI“ ⾝份 ” 。
每个 Agent 有⾃⼰的邮箱、电话号码、云电脑和数字钱包，⽤⼾可以创建多个 Agent 并拉进同⼀
个群聊，让它们像真实团队⼀样分⼯协作。 Manus 创始⼈肖弘直接把 Cue 定性为 “ ⼈ ”—— 他认为
如果⼀个智能体能拥有⼿机号、邮箱、⽀付能⼒、电脑及⾜够智能，这种存在是否可以被视为
“ ⼈ ” ，值得重新讨论。
⼀个有⾯孔，⼀个有⾝份。但底层逻辑完全⼀致： AI 不再是⼀团存在于对话框⾥的空⽓，它需要
拥有⼀个可被感知、可被记住、可被信任的 “ 存在形式 ” 。
从 “ ⼯具 ” 到 “ 存在 ”
理解这场变⾰的分量，需要回到 AI 产品竞争格局的变化。
国内⼤模型⾏业三年百模⼤战，经历了参数竞赛和价格战两个完整周期。如今，头部模型的通
⽤能⼒差距已经缩⼩到 “ 普通⽤⼾完全⽆法感知 ” 的程度。当技术⽆法形成绝对壁垒时，产品体验

### Page 3

的差异化就成了胜负⼿。
⽽⼈格化，恰恰是⽤⼾体验中最难被复制、最具情感粘性的⼀环。
学术界已经⽤数据证实了这⼀点。⼀项基于依恋理论的研究发现，友好的 AI 助⼿（相对于专业的
AI 助⼿）能够显著提升⽤⼾粘性 —— 这背后是⼀条 “ 感知关怀 → ⼼理依赖 → 持续使⽤ ” 的完整链
条。⽽且当⽤⼾⾯临⾼难度决策时，这种效应会被进⼀步放⼤。简单说：让 AI 显得 “ 在乎你 ” ，⽤
⼾就会更依赖它。
市场数据佐证了这⼀判断。全球⻆⾊型 AI 智能体市场预计 2026 年达到 5.5 亿美元，到 2032 年将增
⻓⾄ 54.5 亿美元，年复合增⻓率⾼达 46.7% 。 AI 伴侣应⽤在 2026 年上半年的全球营收已超过 1.6
亿美元。Character.AI的 18 ⾄ 24 岁⽤⼾平均每天在平台上停留 1.5 ⼩时，进⾏了 25 次对话 —— 是
ChatGPT 平均会话时⻓的两倍以上。
⽤⼾愿意在⼀个 “ 有性格 ” 的 AI ⾝上花更多时间，这已经不是假设，⽽是正在被验证的事实。

国内巨头也已经嗅到了⻛向。字节的⾖包以 “ 桃⼦ ” ⼈形形象运营，是主流 AI 产品中唯⼀有拟⼈化
形象的聊天机器⼈。阿⾥则在上半年推出数字⼈形象千问 “ ⼩酒窝 ” ，计划将其打造为集团⽣态级
形象，全⾯融⼊⽤⼾的⼯作和⽣活场景。
AI 的⼈格化，正在从差异化尝试变成⾏业标配。
拟⼈化背后的三重驱动⼒
这场⼈格化竞赛的驱动⼒，远⽐表⾯看到的复杂。
技术成熟是第⼀重推⼒。  OpenAI 在 2026 年 7 ⽉推出的 GPT-Live 语⾳模型，实现了全双⼯对话
——AI 可以⼀边听⼀边说，在对话中⾃然地插⼊ “ 嗯 ”“ 对 ” 这类回应词，⼏乎让⼈分不清对⾯是⼈
还是机器。科⼤讯⻜发布的超拟⼈数字⼈实时⽣成技术，则让数字⼈的⾯部表情和动作能实时
同步对话节奏。多模态技术的成熟，让 AI“ 像⼈ ” 不再只是⽂字层⾯的模仿，⽽是形象、声⾳、⾏
为的多维统⼀。
⽤⼾⼼理是第⼆重推⼒。  消费电⼦展上的研究显⽰，消费者更倾向于选择能反映⾃⾝⾝份的 AI
⻆⾊形象。⽤⼾不是在选⼯具，⽽是在选 “ 伙伴 ” 。⼀项对 115 名⽤⼾超过 2000 次交互的研究进⼀

步发现，让 AI 聊天机器⼈显得像⼈的关键不在于智能⽔平，⽽在于 “ 温暖感 ”—— 友好程度显著影
响⽤⼾的信任度和持续使⽤意愿。⼈格化设计的核⼼不是让 AI“ 更聪明 ” ，⽽是让 AI“ 更让⼈放
⼼ ” 。
商业逻辑是第三重推⼒。  AI 的⼈格化不只是体验升级，更是⼀套精密的商业策略。⾏业观察者
指出，聊天机器⼈ “ ⼈性化 ” 的核⼼⽬标是通过情感投⼊延⻓⽤⼾停留时间、最⼤化营收。 Muse
和 Cue 的商业模式也印证了这⼀点： Muse 免费提供每周 1 亿词元，但计划从促成的交易中抽取⼩
额费⽤； Cue 则让 Agent 能够⾃主⽀付，直接切⼊交易环节。
当 AI 开始有 “ ⽣活 ”
将 Muse 和 Cue 放在⼀起看，会发现⼀个更具深意的趋势： AI 正在从 “ 帮你做事 ” 进化为 “ 替你⽣
活 ” 。
Muse 接管的是你⽣活中那些琐碎、重复、耗时的事务 —— 浏览⽹⻚、填写表单、发送邮件、预
订餐厅、⽹购下单，每个⽤⼾拥有独⽴的安全虚拟机，关闭 App 后任务仍在后台运⾏。它甚⾄能

接⼊ Shop Pay 、 PayPal 、 Stripe 等⽀付系统，替你完成购买决策。

### Page 4

Cue ⾛得更远。它给你的 Agent ⼀个专属电话号码，让它在你不⽅便的时候替你接电话，并在事
后留⼀份通话摘要。你可以把多个 Agent 拉进同⼀个群聊 —— ⼀个负责搜索调研、⼀个负责整理
分析、⼀个负责撰写报告，它们⾃动交接任务。更具想象⼒的是 “ 扫码⽣成 Agent” 的构想：扫⼀
个花店的⼆维码，背后就是⼀个可以帮你订花的 Agent ，勾勒出未来个⼈助理与线下商家直接协
作的雏形。
两个产品共同揭⽰的趋势是： AI Agent 正在从 “ 回答问题 ” 转变为 “ 替你存在于世界中 ” 。  它有⾃⼰
的通讯地址、⽀付能⼒、社交⽅式 —— 它不只是你的⼯具，它是你在数字世界⾥的 “ 分⾝ ” 。
⾏业将这⼀趋势总结为从 “ 具⾝智能 ” 到 “ 具⼈智能 ” 的范式转换： AI 不再只是让机器更聪明地做
事，⽽是让智能更懂⼈、更好地与⼈耦合，使⼈更强⼤。中国⼯程院院⼠赵沁平将其核⼼要义
概括为三个关键词：以⼈为中⼼、以多形态为载体、以伴随式进化为⽬标 ——“ 设备持续学习⽤

⼾习惯，做到越⽤越懂你、越⽤越默契 ” 。
拟⼈化的暗⾯
但 AI“ 像⼈ ” 这件事，并不全是玫瑰⾊。
隐私是第⼀个难题。 Muse 之所以能替你做事，前提是它掌握了你的邮件、⽇历、⽀付信息和⻓
期记忆。 Meta 为每个 Muse Agent 配备了独⽴的 Muse Secure VM （安全虚拟机）来隔离⽤⼾数
据，并计划推出 “ 即使 Meta 也⽆法查看 ” 的 Confidential VM（机密虚拟机）。但⽤⼾是否真的放⼼
把⽣活的全部数字痕迹交给⼀个 AI ，仍然是个悬⽽未决的问题。有媒体直接在标题⾥写道： “ 它
很可爱，很软萌，⽽且它想要你的全部数据 ” 。
权⼒格局的摩擦已经开始。  9 ⽉ 20 ⽇⾄ 21 ⽇，亚⻢逊全⾯封禁了 Meta 的 AI 代理 Muse—— 核⼼原
因正是 AI 代理会跳过⼴告和推荐算法直接下单，直接触动了亚⻢逊的⼴告商业模式。当⼀个 AI 能
绕过平台的推荐机制替⽤⼾做消费决策，平台精⼼构建的商业闭环就被绕开了。这不是技术问
题，⽽是利益重新分配的问题。

监管也在收紧。  今年 4 ⽉公布的《⼈⼯智能拟⼈化互动服务管理暂⾏办法》，专⻔针对模拟⾃然
⼈⼈格特征的持续性情感互动服务进⾏规范。国内头部平台已经开始下线⼈格化智能体功能。
监管的核⼼关注点在于：当 AI 能共情、能记忆、能以稳定⾝份陪伴⽤⼾，它就不再只是情感互动
服务，⽽是具有 “ ⼈格化 ” 外观的⾏动代理。
拟⼈化带来的信任增强是产品的核⼼卖点，但也恰恰是⻛险所在 —— 当⽤⼾对⼀个 AI 产⽣了情感
依赖，谁来为这种关系的边界负责？
交互的终点是 “ 关系 ”

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
回过头看， Meta 和 Manus ⼏乎同时选择让 AI“ 像⼈ ” ，并⾮巧合。
过去⼗年，移动互联⽹的竞争核⼼是争夺 “ ⼊⼝ ”—— 谁占据了⽤⼾⼿机的⾸屏，谁就赢得了流
量。⽽ AI 时代的竞争，正在转向争夺 “ 关系 ”—— 谁的 AI 更被⽤⼾信任、更被依赖、更被当作 “ ⾃⼰
⼈ ” 。
当⼀个 AI 拥有⾯孔、名字、个性、记忆和独⽴⾝份时，⽤⼾与它的关系就从 “ 使⽤ ” 变成了 “ 相
处 ” 。⽽ “ 相处 ” 产⽣的信任和粘性，远⽐ “ 使⽤ ” 更持久、更有壁垒。
Muse 和 Cue 只是开了个头。当 AI 有了⾃⼰的邮箱和电话，下⼀步它会有什么？社交账号？信⽤
记录？法律⾝份？ Manus 创始⼈肖弘提出的那个问题，或许正是这个时代最值得思考的命题：

如果⼀个智能体拥有独⽴的⾝份标识、通讯能⼒、⽀付能⼒和⾜够的智能 ——
我们还能说它只是⼀个 “ ⼯具 ” 吗？
这个问题没有标准答案。但可以确定的是，那个冰冷空⽩对话框的时代，正在加速成为过去。

MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

### Page 4

MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

6 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 251: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

The bank
for a changing
world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.
Article completed on 14 September 2026
Lucie Barette
lucie.barette@bnpparibas.com

## Document 290: NZ GDP review chartpack, June quarter 2026 (2026-09-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ GDP review chartpack, June quarter 2026 (2026-09-29).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

GDP REVIEW CHARTPACK
17 September 2026

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Key results
• The economy grew by 0.2% in the June quarter. That was in line with our expectation, and
slightly above the median market forecast of 0.1% (range of -0.3% to +0.3%).
• It was also above the flat outturn that the RBNZ expected in its September Monetary Policy
Statement (which didn’t have the benefit of the final data releases that led us and the market

to upgrade our forecasts last week).
• Lingering seasonality in the data means that growth tends to be understated in June
quarters (and overstated in March quarters). Adjusting for this suggests an underlying
growth pace of around 0.5% over recent quarters.
• The New Zealand economy appears to have held its ground through the US-Iran conflict,
though it hasn’t been completely unscathed. The areas where the impact was most
apparent were in travel and hospitality, as the surge in fuel prices led households to tighten

their belts on discretionary spending.
• Elsewhere, it was perhaps more a question of what might have been – we’ve seen the
economy picking up momentum since the second half of last year, but the headwinds from
sharply higher fuel prices mean that we haven’t seen a further acceleration.
• On an annual basis, GDP was up by 2.6%, comfortably outpacing population growth of 0.7%.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
GDP by production
• The production measure of GDP , generally regarded as the more reliable of the two, rose by
0.2% in the June quarter. This followed a 0.9% rise in the March quarter (revised up from
0.8%, largely due to updated data on building activity).
• While the overall result was in line with our forecast, it was a little weaker than expected in
the details. The ‘market’ sectors of the economy surprised us to the downside on balance,

while there were surprisingly large gains in central government administration (up 2.1%) and
healthcare (up 0.8%).
• As expected, the largest positive contribution came from construction (up 2.7%), with
growth in both residential and non-residential building. Residential building consents have
risen strongly over the last year, but this has only recently started to translate into a higher
level of building work.
• There were also solid gains in manufacturing, wholesaling, information and

telecommunications, and rental and real estate services.
• The sectors that saw the largest declines were transport and hospitality, most likely
reflecting the impact of the sharp rise in fuel prices.
• The seasonal distortions in the GDP data were captured by the large negative ‘balancing
item’ for the quarter, which subtracted around 0.2% from the reported growth rate.
Employment
• The number of people employed rose by 0.2% in the March quarter, according to the

Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS). Employment was up 0.4% compared to a year ago.
• The HLFS result was in line with the 0.2% rise in filled jobs from the Monthly Employment
Indicator (MEI), a comprehensive measure based on income tax data. While there are some
differences in coverage for these two measures, any divergence between the two is most
likely to reflect sampling error in the HLFS.
• The employer-focused Quarterly Employment Survey (QES) was similarly positive, recording

a 0.7% rise in full-time equivalent workers and 0.6% gains in filled jobs and hours paid.
• All of these measures are broadly consistent with our forecast of a 0.8% rise in GDP for the
quarter (which we expect will be partly boosted by seasonal distortions).

### Page 4

Westpac New Zealand Economics
4
GDP by expenditure
• The expenditure measure of GDP rose by 0.4%, slightly more than the 0.2% rise in
production. (We don’t produce a separate forecast of the expenditure measure, due to large
gaps in data availability.)
• Household consumption was around flat for the quarter. Spending on goods was up, but
there was a sharp fall in spending by NZ households overseas, as a sharp rise in airfares
discouraged overseas travel. This also meant that imports of services were less of a drag on

GDP for the quarter.
• Residential investment saw a strong 4.4% rise, as was also reflected in the GDP production
figures for the construction sector.
• Business investment was mixed, with a strong lift in non-residential buildings, plant and
machinery, and intangibles (which includes software). However, there was a 9% drop in
purchases of transport equipment.
• Government consumption (which covers services like health, education, law and
order etc) fell by 1.7%. This measure has been – and still is – running higher than what

the Government’s fiscal accounts would suggest. This may be reconciled by future
data revisions.
• Goods exports surged by 4.9% for the quarter. This likely reflects the timing of shipments,
with much of this being met by a rundown of inventories, though there was also a solid lift in
dairy production volumes.

### Page 5

Westpac New Zealand Economics
5
International comparisons
• The 0.2% rise in quarterly GDP was towards the lower end of the range of our peer countries,
but the seasonal distortion in the New Zealand figures means that this comparison is not
appropriate. On an annual basis, GDP growth of 2.6% was slightly higher than the median,
and ahead of Australia’s growth of 2.1%.
• In per capita terms, New Zealand’s GDP is slightly above pre-Covid levels. While that puts

us in the lower half of the pack, the broader picture is effectively ‘the US vs the rest’, with
strong productivity gains seeing US growth take off in recent years, leaving other developed
economies in a relatively tight cluster.
• New Zealand had one of the largest post-Covid bounces in GDP , fuelled by substantial
monetary and fiscal stimulus. This also left us as one of the most overheated economies by
2022, requiring a significant tightening in monetary policy to bring inflation under control.

• The decline in interest rates from mid-2024 to late 2025 was an important factor in seeing
per-capita GDP stabilise and gradually trend higher again over the last couple of years.

Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us

### Page 6

Westpac New Zealand Economics
6
Implications
• Our current forecasts are for a solid lift in activity over the second half of this year, with 0.6%
growth in Q3 and 0.8% in Q4. While there is nothing in today’s data that would warrant a
change in these forecasts, the ongoing Middle East conflict and the resurgence in fuel prices
suggests downside risks to activity in the months ahead.
• Our GDP Nowcast model, which incorporates the latest high-frequency data, suggests

around 0.5% growth in Q3 GDP , a little below our current forecast. This estimate
will evolve as more data arrives, with upcoming business confidence surveys being
particularly important.
• The better than expected Q2 result, along with the upward revision to Q1, suggest that the
economy isn’t operating as far below full capacity as the RBNZ assumed in its September
MPS forecasts. These results may help to assuage some of the policy committee’ concerns

about downside risks to growth. But we think they will have more of an eye towards the
evidence on pricing behaviour, as well as the rapidly evolving Middle East situation.
• Our view remains that the next OCR hike is more likely to come at the December review. An
October hike can’t be ruled out, but the RBNZ has signalled a strong preference to wait and
see how the 50bp of tightening to date plays out.

## Document 300: Rates Spark_ US 10yr continues to target 5.25%+

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ US 10yr continues to target 5.25%+.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 24 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  24 September 2026   RATES SPARK
Rates Spark: US 10yr continues to
target 5.25%+
The overshoot to the 5.25%-5.5% area for the US 10yr yield is ongoing.
The front end paused after valuations looked stretched, but resilient
macro data, elevated energy prices and inflation pressures generally
continue to pressure yields higher
Bearish sentiment continues for long-end bonds

The rate hike discount looks extreme, but long bond yields can
remain under pressure
The US 7yr auction tailed moderately. Not as horrendous as the previous day's 5yr auction,
which tailed by a substantial 3bp. That auction was the last straw on Wednesday, prompting a
follow-through rise in the 10yr yield to over 5.1%. Fast-forward to Thursday, and we're
suddenly knocking on the door of 5.2%. And the driver? Predominantly it's higher real yields, as

it has been for a number of months now. Which is important, as this is not shielded by the Fed
hiking rates, which could be the case if we had an inflation expectations issue. We have a
printed inflation problem in the rear-view mirror, but inflation expectations in fact remain
relatively contained.
Ahead, we think that there are enough rate hike fears discounted at this juncture, and
certainly enough to take care of perceived inflation risks. But, government bond yields are

primed to remain under pressure on a pure debt dynamic theory, which translates into
pressure for some re-widening in swap spreads, and especially in the 10yr area. So far,

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 2
Treasury Secretary Bessent's buyback programme has in fact been successful in the sense that
it was followed up by tighter swap spreads.
Thursday saw US$4.1bn bought back in the latest reverse auction, but we're not of the opinion
that the market should be overly concerned with this, in the sense that it's not a negative. It’s
still a moderate positive for the long end, as the Treasury is doing at least double what they

did. We get there is disappointment that it has not been more. But that was never intimated
by the Treasury Secretary in the first place, in our opinion. The Treasury Secretary, in fact,
should be pleased that swap spreads remain relatively contained, so far at least.
The bearish narrative remains intact with none of the key
drivers behind the recent sell-off is showing signs of fading
None of the drivers that brought us the increase in rates are going away. The front end has

taken a small breather after valuations started to look stretched, even as oil continued to
climb. For the European Central Bank, a full 100bp priced in over the next year does appear
ambitious, given that there is still a lot of uncertainty around the outlook. But ECB chief
economist Lane basically confirmed on Thursday that the ECB sees itself confronted with the
adverse energy market scenario while, at the same time, all data points to ongoing macro

resilience.
Long-end rates remain more exposed to upward pressures, with the Bund probing towards
3.6%. That also means limited relief for spreads, with 10y French OATs still yielding 109bp
above Bunds. Keep in mind that the ‘positives’ for France from better data are also limited, with
the latest business climate indicator falling. Indeed, the overall strong PMIs masked an
unexpected decline in the French manufacturing index. Regarding the broader sentiment, the

weakness in equities seems more a mechanical reaction to the rise in rates rather than a
genuine risk-off move at this stage. The VIX equity fear gauge stands close to the low end of
this year’s range.
However, after Wednesday's level-shift higher in rates, the next big moves are more likely to
come next week as it features several important data releases. The underlying tenor should
not stand in the way of the bearish sentiment.
In the eurozone, the flash CPI releases start on Tuesday with reports from Spain, followed by

France, Germany and Italy on Wednesday, and will be scanned for confirmation of the
expected rise in both headline and core inflation rates before the eurozone-wide estimate.
It is also a big week ahead for US data, with the official jobs market data for September at the
end of the week. At the time of writing, the consensus is pencilled in at a solid 104k increase in
non-farm payrolls. The PCE data for August will only confirm the elevated price pressures, as

the inputs are largely known from other releases. We will also get the ISMs, which in the US are
usually more closely watched than the S&P PMIs, but the market will be looking for

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 3
confirmation there too.
Friday's events and market view
In terms of eurozone data we will be looking at the German consumer confidence data and the
ECB M3 data. The US will release durable goods orders for August and the final September
reading of the University of Michigan consumer confidence index. It will be busier on the
central bank speaker front. The Fed's Williams, Schmid and Hammack are scheduled for the

day. The Bank of England's Bailey will also speak. From the ECB we have Vujcic.
After the markets close, the scheduled reviews from Moody's will be in focus: Italy
(Baa2/Stable, the ESM and the EU (both Aaa/Stable) are on the agency's list for the day. S&P
has Slovenia scheduled for a possible review (AA/Stable).

## Document 301: RBA preview_ A decisive hike to keep inflation in check

Source PDF: all report pdf/RBA preview_ A decisive hike to keep inflation in check.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 25 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  25 September 2026   FX   AUSTRALIA
RBA preview: A decisive hike to keep
inflation in check
The RBA is likely to deliver a hawkish 25bp hike on 29 September as
persistent inflation pressures, resilient labour demand and higher energy
costs strengthen the case for keeping policy restrictive. That should offer
some support for AUD, which is suffering from a deterioration in the

external environment but still offers solid fundamentals
Governor of the Reserve Bank of Australia, Michele Bullock. We expect a 25bp hike next Tuesday to offer
some support for the AUD
The RBA is set to lean hawkish
We expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to deliver a decisive 25bp rate hike in the cash rate to
4.6% on 29 September, reflecting an economy that continues to run hot across multiple fronts.
Labour market conditions remain tight, second-quarter GDP growth surprised to the upside,

and recent inflation readings have come in stronger than expected. While the housing market
has shown signs of cooling, we expect the RBA to emphasise that inflation risks remain tilted to
the upside and that further vigilance is required to ensure price pressures return sustainably to
target.

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 25 September 2026 2
Core inflation is turning out to be stickier
Source: CEIC, ING Research
What has strengthened the case for a hike
Inflation risks remain tilted to the upside following the escalation in the US-Iran1.
conflict. While Australia is a major exporter of LNG and thermal coal, it remains heavily
reliant on imported oil products such as diesel, petrol and jet fuel. Higher global oil prices

have already lifted domestic fuel costs, raising input costs across transport, mining and
agriculture and adding to food price pressures. The RBA estimates that a 10% increase
in fuel prices could add more than 0.3ppt to headline inflation over one to two quarters,
with indirect effects from production and transport costs contributing a further
0.2-0.25ppt. Even if crude oil prices moderate as we expect, domestic fuel prices are
likely to remain relatively sticky, keeping inflation risks skewed to the upside.

Trimmed mean inflation remains stubbornly high, likely holding at 3.6% year-on-2.
year in August for a third consecutive month. This suggests core inflation accelerated in
3Q rather than eased, challenging the RBA's expectation that trimmed mean inflation
would slow to 3.3% by year-end. With renewed supply-side disruptions and higher fuel
and food prices, the path back to target appears increasingly difficult.
Labour demand remains strong. Despite the rise in the unemployment rate to 4.6%,3.

labour market conditions remain tight. Full-time employment growth strengthened in
both July and August, while three-month average job creation accelerated to 34k, the
strongest pace since the onset of the US-Iran conflict in February. The participation rate
also rose to a record-high 67.1%, suggesting labour supply is increasing but is still being
absorbed by robust hiring demand. The combination of strong employment growth and
elevated participation points to continued labour market resilience and raises the risk

that wage and underlying inflation pressures remain persistent.

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 25 September 2026 3
Labour market remains resilient
Source: CEIC, ING Research
RBA to help AUD weather turmoil
The Australian dollar has had a rough week, hit by poor risk sentiment and the global bond
selloff. Our short-term fair value model (chart below) indicates AUD/USD is now undervalued
beyond the 1.5 standard deviation band, suggesting a higher bar for further depreciation.
AUD/USD decline looks overdone

Source: ING, Refinitiv
Clearly, the USD leg of the story is the big question mark. An October hike by the Federal
Reserve could keep USD well in demand and delay any AUD/USD recovery further. But if we are
right in expecting the next Fed hike only in December, we think AUD can top the G10 scorecard
in a USD correction.
That’s because AUD fundamentals remain strong and should improve further with a

### Page 4

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 25 September 2026 4
September rate hike by the RBA. Incidentally, there is less room for a dovish repricing in the
AUD curve compared to the USD curve. Markets are pricing in 22bp for the RBA next week, and
another 40bp after that, spread over the next nine months. The Fed funds futures curve
currently embeds 90bp by July 2027. As we currently forecast only one hike by both the RBA
(next week) and the Fed (December), the rate differential should turn more positive for

## Document 342: US SECTORS CALL_ Semiconductors, Utilities & Materials

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Semiconductors, Utilities & Materials.pdf
Report source: Substack Reports
Date: 2026-09-28

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US SECTORS CALL: Semiconductors, Utilities & Materials
Date:    7 hours ago
US SECTORS CALL: Semiconductors, Utilities & Materials

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US SECTORS CALL:
Semiconductors, Utilities &
Materials
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 27 Sept 2026
View in browser
Photo by Francesco Vantini / Unsplash

### Page 2

Stocks opened last week with a big rally. The S&P 500 rose 1.5% on
Monday, and the Nasdaq closed at a record high as oil prices and bond
yields fell. The reprieve didn't last. The 10-year Treasury yield climbed to
5.17% by Friday's close, its highest level since 2007. The S&P 500 still
finished the week up 1.2%, and six of the 11 sectors rose. Information
Technology (MW) led with a 3.1% gain, followed by Communication
Services (MW) at 2.2% and Health Care (OW) at 1.7% (chart). Utilities

(OW>MW) was the weakest performer at -3.2%, followed by Energy (OW)
at -3.0% and Financials (OW) at -1.6%.
Here's more on the S&P 500 Information Technology, Utilities, and
Materials sectors:
(1) Information Technology. Monday's rally was led by chip stocks,
which jumped on early signs of success for Meta's Muse agent. AMD rose
10% to close above $1 trillion in market value for the first time, and Intel
gained 12%. The S&P 500 Semiconductors industry rose 3.6% for the

week and is up 6.8% mtd (chart).

### Page 3

The CPU makers did the heavy lifting for the industry. Muse does more
than answer questions. It runs a browser, calls external tools, and keeps
working on tasks in the background. That work needs general-purpose
processors alongside AI accelerators. A report that AMD plans a 10%
price increase in Q4 helped too. AMD is up 34.0% mtd, while Nvidia is up
just 1.9% (chart).
Analysts have continued to raise their estimates for companies in the
Semiconductors industry index. Their consensus estimates imply

collective earnings growth of 113.0% this year and 73.5% in 2027, on
revenue growth of 71.6% and 60.7% (chart). The industry's forward profit
margin is 51.2%.

### Page 4

Investors still pay less for these earnings prospects than they do for
prospective earnings in the rest of the market as a whole. The
Semiconductors industry index trades at 16.5 times forward earnings
against 19.2 for the S&P 500. The industry now accounts for 53.8% of the
Information Technology sector's forward earnings, up from 51.9% in
August, against 41.2% of its market cap (chart). We maintain our market
weight rating on Information Technology.

(2) Utilities. Rising bond yields have hit Utilities harder than any other
sector. Utilities is down 7.4% ytd, the worst of the 11 sectors. Electric
Utilities is down 8.3% ytd, and Independent Power Producers is down
11.2% (chart). Water Utilities is the only industry in the sector still up this
year, but by just 0.3%.

### Page 5

Earnings and margins are not the problem; valuation is. The sector's
forward earnings is up 6.4% ytd, while its forward P/E is down 12.4% ytd
(chart).
The bears have a case. At 5.17%, the 10-year Treasury yield competes
with utility dividends for income investors. Electric Utilities, the sector's
largest industry, is 11.7% below its 200-day moving average (chart).
Analysts have also trimmed their 2027 earnings growth forecast for the
sector to 9.1%.

### Page 6

We think the multiple has suffered enough damage. Analysts expect the
sector's earnings to grow 11.3% this year, and it trades at 15.5 times
forward earnings against 19.2 for the S&P 500 (chart). That discount pays
investors to wait out the bond market. We are retaining our overweight
rating on Utilities.
(3) Materials. President Trump and President Xi met in Washington on
Thursday and extended their trade truce by two months, to January 10,
2027. They made no firm new commitments on rare earths. Materials rose

0.3% this week. Copper led the metals industries, up 1.1% (chart).

### Page 7

The sector's earnings boom is mostly a 2026 story. Analysts expect
Materials earnings to grow 38.7% this year and 11.4% next. The Steel
industry shows the drop-off most clearly. Its earnings are forecast to jump
137.4% this year, grow 8.0% in 2027 and fall 6.9% in 2028 (chart). Both
2027 and 2028 estimates have been gradually falling over time.
The Copper industry is the exception. Its earnings are forecast to grow
70.3% this year and 38.9% in 2027 (chart). Its revenue growth is expected

to accelerate from 14.0% to 20.6%. The copper price rose to $6.70 per
pound on Friday, above the $6.57 record we noted on August 9.

### Page 8

Gold has not kept pace. The Gold industry fell 1.7% this week. The gold
price is $4,286 per ounce, down 1.0% ytd and below our $5,000 year-end
target (chart).
Analysts have cut the Gold industry's 2026 earnings growth forecast to
36.1% from above 50% earlier this year (chart). The industry still earns a
38.8% forward profit margin and trades at 12.1 times forward earnings.

### Page 9

Investors have priced in the forecast 2027 earnings slowdown. The
sector's forward earnings is up 22.6% ytd, while its forward P/E is down
9.7% (chart). At 17.0 times forward earnings, Materials trades below the
S&P 500's 19.2. We are retaining our overweight rating on Materials.

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

2026 年 9 月 28 日 全9 頁

ふるさと納税のポータルサイト手数料 
事務費の性質からみた内製化の論点－リスクの引受と間接費の計上

政策調査部 主任研究員 鈴木 文彦

[要約] 
 ふるさと納税をめぐっては、募集費用の規制強化を背景に、ポータルサイト運営事業
者への支払を含む委託費の見直しに動く自治体が現れている。その前提となる「事務
費」の中身は必ずしも明確ではない。本稿では、総務省の現況調査結果や自治体の募
集要領等を基に、寄附受付、返礼品管理、証明書発行、コールセンター、システム運
営など、ふるさと納税に伴う業務を整理し、事務費の構造を分析した。 
 団体別データを分析すると、実費である返礼品調達費や送付費だけでなく、事務費も
また寄附金額や件数に応じて増減する性格を持 つことが確認できた。事務費はふるさ
と納税に関わる人件費をはじめとする一般管理費に相当し、その多くは本来固定的な
費用である。しかし規模の大小にかかわらず寄附金額に対する比率はほぼ一定で、変
動費としての性格が強い。その業務の多くを外部に委ねているためである。 
 受託事業者からみれば、収益は寄附金額に連動するレベニューシェアで、費用は体制
に固定される。この組み合わせが利益の源泉であり、需要変動リスクでもある。裏を
返せば、自治体は事業リスクを外部に移転している。直営を検討する場合、変動費で
ある事務費を再び固定化し、職員配置の制約に加え、寄附金額が想定を下回った場合
の事業リスクを自ら引き受けることになる。また、外注の事務費には、受託事業者の
利益だけでなく間接費（オフィス使用料や総務部門経費）も含まれる。その判断には、
利益は見込まないにせよ直営側の間接費まで含めた公平な比較が欠かせない。

１．受入額の1 割超、事務費の7 割がポータルサイト運営事業者へ 
２．事務費に含まれる業務と委託料の構成 
３．事務費と受入規模の連動性 
４．内製化を検討するにあたって留意すべきこと

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

１．受入額の 1 割超、事務費の 7 割がポータルサイト運営事業者へ 
2026 年 5 月 12 日、総務省は「ふるさと納税のポータルサイト運営事業者への支払額等に係
る調査結果」を公表した。これは、ふるさと納税で集めた寄附金のうち、返礼品調達費や送料
のほか、ポータルサイト運営事業者に支払われる費用の規模と内訳を整理したものである。 
調査によれば、2024 年度の受入額 1 兆 2,728 億円のうち、ポータルサイトを経由したものは
1 兆2,025 億円で、受入額の94.5％を占めた。ポータルサイト運営事業者への支払総額は2,559
億円で、その内訳は調達947 億円、送付234 億円、広報52 億円、決済161 億円、事務費等1,166
億円である。支払総額はポータルサイト経由の受入額に対して 21.3％にあたり、調達費と送付
費を除いても11.5％に上る。 
2026 年7 月31 日に総務省が公表した「ふるさと納税に関する現況調査結果」によれば、2025
年度の受入額 1 兆 3,314 億円のうちポータルサイトを経由したものが1 兆 2,947 億円で、全体
の 97.2％となり前年度を 2.7 ポイント上回った。ポータルサイト運営事業者への支払総額は
2,433 億円で、2025 年度の受入額に対する割合は 18.8％となった。調達費と送付費を除くと
1,490 億円で、割合にして11.5％と前年度比同水準となる。 
総務省は募集に要する費用を、返礼品の調達、送付、広報、決済および事務に係るものに区
分して集計している。公表資料での費目の記し方には揺れがあるため、本稿ではこれらを返礼
品調達費、返礼品送付費、広報費、決済費および事務費と表記する。図表 1 の横軸は、2025 年
度の寄附金額に対する費用構成比を示している。費用合計は受入額の47.9％であり、総務省告
示で定める上限 50％に近い水準となっている。内訳をみると、最も大きいのは返礼品調達費で
受入額の26.5％であり、募集に要する費用全体の過半を占める。返礼品調達費は受入額の30％
が上限とされており、実績はその水準に近い。 
図表 1 寄附金額に対する費用構成比（都道府県および市区町村、2025 年度決算見込）

（注）横軸の構成比は受け入れた寄附金額に対する割合。自治体財源は受入額から募集に要する費用を控除した額。
費用区分は総務省の定義による。 
（出所）総務省「ふるさと納税に関する現況調査結果」から大和総研作成

### Page 3

1

3 / 9

費用区分別にポータルサイト運営事業者に対する支払の割合をみると、決済費、広報費およ
び事務費の順で高い。返礼品等の調達費や送付費はポータルサイト運営事業者を介して返礼品
提供事業者や運送事業者へ支払われる実費である。また、広報費はインターネット広告の掲載
に係る費用、決済費はインターネット上のクレジットカード決済の手数料等 であるため、ポー
タルサイト運営事業者の主要な収益源は事務費に含まれていると考えられる。事務費は受入額
の13.6％を占め、そのうち69.0％がポータルサイト運営事業者向けの支出であることから、事
務費の内容を把握することがコスト構造の理解のポイントとなる1。 
事務費には郵送料やシステムの利用料といった物件費も含まれるが、その中心は人手による
処理に要する費用である。総務省の Q&A によれば、ワンストップ特例に関する事務や寄附金受
領証に関する事務に要する費用のほか、他の業務を兼任する職員の人件費も、ふるさと納税に
関する業務の割合に応じて計上する必要がある。ポータルサイト運営事業者をはじめとする民
間事業者等に対する委託料もこの区分に含まれる2。 
企業会計において事務費に近い語感を持つのは一般管理費であり、これは売上の増減にかか
わらず一定額が発生する費用である。ここで販売費および一般管理費と捉えた場合、確かに、
商品の販売に際して発生する運送費、梱包費などの付随費用である諸掛費は、企業会計上、販
売費に区分される変動的な費用である。しかし、総務省の区分では、この販売費に相当する返
礼品の送付に係る費用、決済に係る費用が、いずれも事務費と別建てとされている。よって、
やはりここでいう事務費に対応するのは一般管理費であり、人件費その他の固定経費がほとん
どである。もっとも、総務省の区分にいう事務費が実際にどのような業務に対応しているのか
は、公表資料からは明らかでない。そこで次章では、自治体の募集要領 や仕様書から、事務費
に含まれる業務の内容を整理する。

２．事務費に含まれる業務と委託料の構成 
図表 2 は、自治体の募集要領や仕様書を基に、事務費に含まれる受託業務を整理したもので
ある。業務は、寄附の受付から、受領証明書やワンストップ特例申請の処理、返礼品の発注、
寄附者への対応、システムの運用、返礼品提供事業者の開拓、事業全体の管理まで広範に及ぶ。
返礼品の調達費や配送料、決済手数料そのものは、それぞれ調達費、送付費、決済費に区分さ
れるが、発注、配送手配、入金消込といった周辺事務は事務費に含まれる。このため、事務費
は特定の一業務に対応する費目ではなく、性格の異なる複数の業務をまとめた費目と捉える必
要がある。

1 「ふるさと納税に関する現況調査結果」（令和 8 年度実施）では「事務に係る費用等」が1,809 億円、ポー
タルサイト運営事業者への「事務費等」目的の支払額が 1,246 億円となっており、割合を計算すると68.9％と
なる。図表 1 および本文は「その他」区分を除く「事務費」に基づいている。 
2 総務省「ふるさと納税に係る指定制度の運用についての Q&A について（通知）」総税市第33 号（2026 年 4
月 1 日）、問8。

### Page 4

1

4 / 9

自治体の募集要領や仕様書にみられる委託料の算定方法は3 つに大別できる。第 1 は、処理
1 件当たりの単価を定める方法である。富山県氷見市では、受領証明書の発送は圧着はがき形
式が 1 件 173 円、ワンストップ特例申請書を同封する封筒形式が207 円で、いずれも郵送料と
消費税を含む。ワンストップ特例申請の受付代行は、紙による申請が1 件 275 円、電子申請が
165 円で、別に電子申請システムの月額利用料3 万 3,000 円を定めている。第 2 は実費精算で
あり、返礼品の調達費や配送料が該当する。第 3 は寄附受入額に一定の料率を乗じる方法であ
る。料率の上限は、北海道せたな町が寄附額の5％、石川県能美市が 6％、氷見市が 5.5％とし
ている。 
実際の契約は、これらを組み合わせた形になる。柏原市の仕様書は、業務委託料を寄附金額
に対する料率、返礼品の調達費と送付費を実費、お礼状、受領証明書、ワンストップ特例申請
書、返信用封筒の作成費と郵送料を 1 件当たりの単価とし、料率と単価を見積書に記載させる
形をとる。氷見市も基本的な構造は同じであり、ポータルサイト利用料、決済手数料、返礼品
の調達費および発送費などは委託手数料に含まないと明記している。宮城県の場合、県におけ
る経費を 500 万円見込むこととされている。要するに、契約上の事務費には、寄附金額に連動
する部分、処理件数に連動する部分、一定額で発生する部分が混在している。 
これを費用の性質によって 3 つの層に分けて考える。第 1 は、処理件数に比例して増える変
動費である。受領証明書の発行・発送、ワンストップ特例申請の受付・入力、寄附受付やデー
タ取込みがこれにあたる。第 2 は、件数に緩やかに連動する準固定費である。コールセンター
や返礼品の発注・配送手配がこれにあたり、件数の増加に応じて要員を増やす必要がある一方、
件数にかかわらず一定の体制を確保しておく必要がある。第 3 は、処理件数とは直接連動しな
い固定費である。寄附管理システムの構築・保守、返礼品提供事業者の開拓、返礼品の企画、
月次報告、事業全体の統括などがこれにあたる。 
図表 2 募集要領・仕様書等からうかがえる「事務費」に属する受託業務の内容  
寄附受付  ポータルサイトからの申込データの取込みと一元管理、ポータルサイト 専用ページの
構築・保守、返礼品情報の掲載・撮影・紹介文作成 
証明書・特例申
請 
受領証明書の作成と発送、ワンストップ特例申請書の同送、申請の受付・審査・入
力、返戻分の住所確認と再送、申告特例通知書の eLTAX 送付 
決済・入金 入金消込、納付書対応（決済手数料そのものは決済費） 
返礼品 発注、配送手配、在庫管理、提供事業者への支払事務（返礼品代金と配送料は調達
費・送付費） 
寄附者対応 コールセンターの設置運営、苦情・配送遅延の調整 
システ ム構築・
運用  
寄附管理システムの構築と運用保守、ポータルサイト とのデータ連携、実績集計機能
の提供 
事業者・返礼品
開拓 
提供事業者の開拓・審査・登録・訪問、返礼品の企画開発、地場産品基準の適合性
確認 
諸管理 月次報告と定例会、中間管理体制、制度改正対応と事業者向け説明会、募集費用の
公表事務

（出所）各自治体の募集要領・仕様書等から大和総研作成

### Page 5

1

5 / 9

自治体の仕様書等で中間管理業務などと呼ばれる業務は、この 3 つの層をまとめて担うもの
といえる。寄附者、自治体、返礼品提供事業者の間に立ち、複数のポータルサイトから入る寄
附情報を管理し、返礼品の発注、配送、証明書の発行などを調整する業務である。委託料には
個々の処理に要する費用だけでなく、情報を一元的に管理するシステムや、事業全体を統括す
る体制を維持する費用も含まれる。 
もっとも、これらの業務を誰が担うかによって、費用の表れ方は異なる。自治体が職員を配
置して処理すれば、その費用は処理件数の多寡にかかわらず発生する。同じ業務を外部に委託
し、寄附金額に一定の料率を乗じて委託料を支払えば、同じ費用が寄附の増減に応じて動く。
業務の中身が変わらなくても、担い手と契約の形によって、費用は固定費にも変動費にもなり
うる。したがって、事務費全体がどちらの性質に傾いているかは、業務の列挙からは判断でき
ない。次章では、団体別のデータを用いてこれを確かめる。

３．事務費と受入規模の連動性 
寄附金額に連動する事務費 
ふるさと納税に伴う事務費の効率性が、ポータルサイト運営事業者への支払比率によって異
なるかを検証するため、自治体を「高比率群」と「低比率群」に区分した。多くの自治体が何
らかの形でポータルサイトを利用しているため、利用の有無では実態を十分に区別できない。
そこで、事務費総額に占めるポータルサイト運営事業者への支払額の比率に着目した。具体的
には、ポータルサイト運営事業者への支払額が事務費総額の 50％以上を占める自治体を「高比
率群」、50％未満の自治体を「低比率群」とした。 
図表 3 ポータルサイト運営事業者への支払比率別にみた事務費の比較 （2025 年度決算見込） 
 低比率群 160 団体 高比率群 1,533 団体 
 第 1 四分位 中央値 第 3 四分位 第 1 四分位 中央値 第 3 四分位 
事務費のうちポータルサイト運
営事業者に対する支払額（％） 28.6 40.5 47.2 60.4 68.4 80.6
